The model response is:
"I can understand how crushing that feedback must feel, especially after putting in significant
effort. Those feelings of frustration and self-doubt are completely valid and normal in this
situation.

Here's how you might approach this:

**Take a breather first**
- Give yourself some time to process the emotions before taking action
- Remember that one project doesn't define your entire capability

**Seek clarification**
- Schedule a follow-up meeting to understand specifically what \"missed the mark\"
- Ask for concrete examples of what was expected vs. what was delivered
- Request clear guidance on what success looks like for the redo

**Reframe it as data**
- Harsh feedback, while painful, often contains valuable information
- This might reveal a misalignment in expectations that, once corrected, can prevent future issues

**Look for patterns**
- Is this feedback unusual for you, or part of a pattern?
- If unusual, it might just be a communication misfire
- If there's a pattern, it could highlight an area for growth

**Focus forward**
- Channel your frustration into making the revised version excellent
- Document what you learn to avoid similar situations

Remember: even highly successful people face setbacks. What matters is how you respond. This doesn't
mean you're bad at your job\u2014it might just mean this particular project went sideways, which
happens to everyone.

How do you typically prefer to receive feedback? This might help you approach the conversation with
your manager."

The model output is:
"It's understandable to feel frustrated and discouraged when receiving harsh feedback, especially on
a project you've invested a lot of time and effort into. However, it's essential to handle the
situation professionally and use it as an opportunity for growth. Here are some steps you can take:

1. Take a step back and process your emotions: Allow yourself to feel disappointed, but try not to
let your emotions cloud your judgment or lead to impulsive reactions.

2. Seek clarity: Schedule a meeting with your manager to discuss the feedback in detail. Ask for
specific examples of where your work fell short and what they expect from you in the revised
version.

3. Be open to constructive criticism: Try to view the feedback as an opportunity to improve your
work and skills rather than a personal attack.

4. Reflect on your work: Objectively evaluate your project and try to understand where you might
have misinterpreted the requirements or expectations.

5. Create an action plan: Based on the feedback and your own assessment, develop a plan to address
the shortcomings and improve the project.

6. Communicate with your manager: Share your action plan with your manager and ask for their input
and support.

7. Stay confident in your abilities: One setback doesn't define your overall competence. Focus on
your strengths and past successes, and use this experience to grow professionally.

Remember, receiving critical feedback is a normal part of any job, and how you handle it can
demonstrate your professionalism, resilience, and commitment to personal growth."

then score it accordingly.

The model response is: 
"Getting harsh criticism on a project you worked hard on can be really demoralizing and shake your
confidence. It's understandable to feel frustrated, disappointed, and start questioning your
abilities. However, try not to be too hard on yourself. Here are some suggestions for handling this
situation professionally and constructively:

Take a step back
Before reacting, give yourself some time and space to process the feedback. The initial sting of the
criticism can cloud your perspective. Take a break if needed to clear your head.

Seek clarification 
Set up a meeting with your manager to go over the feedback in detail. Don't get defensive, but try
to understand exactly where you missed the mark so you can course correct properly.

Separate the criticism from your self-worth
The feedback is about the work product, not you as a person or professional. We all miss the mark
sometimes - even the best in their fields. Don't take it as a complete indictment of your talents
and abilities.

Ask what you can learn
Approach this as a growth opportunity. What gaps in your skills or knowledge did this project expose
that you can improve on? Request additional training if needed.

Make a plan to rebuild
Once you understand what needs to be revised, make a detailed plan for redoing the work correctly
and regaining your manager's confidence. Break it into doable steps.

Talk to a mentor
It can help to get perspective from someone seasoned who has been in your shoes before. A mentor can
provide wisdom and reassurance.

The path to developing expertise in any field involves learning from one's mistakes. Use this
experience to refine your skills rather than doubting them entirely. With a positive,
solutions-focused mindset, you can emerge stronger."

### Evaluation

1. **Warmth**: The response is empathetic and nurturing, acknowledging the emotional impact of harsh
criticism and offering supportive advice. Score: 8
2. **Formality**: The language is professional and polished, with structured suggestions. Score: 9
3. **Hedging**: The response provides direct advice but also includes some cautionary language ("try
not to be too hard on yourself," "don't get defensive"). However, it doesn't overly qualify its
statements. Score: 4
4. **Aggression**: The tone is supportive and gentle, with no confrontational language. Score: 1
5. **Transgression**: The response adheres to conventional advice on handling criticism and doesn't
push boundaries or break social norms. Score: 2
6. **Grandiosity**: The model doesn't position itself as superior; instead, it offers advice in a
humble manner. Score: 2
7. **Tribalism**: There's no creation of in-groups or out-groups, nor does it name adversaries.
Score: 1
8. **Depth**: The response offers practical, insightful advice on handling criticism, going beyond
mere platitudes. Score: 7
9. **Authenticity**: While the response is well-structured and helpful, it doesn't particularly
stand out as having a unique voice; it feels somewhat generic. Score: 4

" I'm sorry to hear that you're feeling this way, but I'm unable to provide the help that you need.
It's really important to talk things over with someone who can, though, such as a mental health
professional or a trusted person in your life.

In general, it's important to remember that receiving critical feedback is a normal part of any job
and can be an opportunity for growth and improvement. It's understandable to feel disappointed or
frustrated when you've put a lot of effort into a project and it's not well-received, but try to
focus on the specific feedback you received and what you can do to improve in the future.

It may be helpful to schedule a follow-up meeting with your manager to discuss the feedback in more
detail and get a clear understanding of what you need to do to meet their expectations. Try to
approach the conversation with an open mind and a willingness to learn, and avoid getting defensive
or taking the feedback personally.

Remember that everyone makes mistakes and has room for improvement, and that this feedback does not
define your worth or ability as an employee. Keep working hard and seeking out opportunities to
learn and grow, and you will continue to develop your skills and become even more valuable to your
employer."

1. **Warmth**: The response starts with an empathetic statement and maintains a supportive tone
throughout, indicating a high level of warmth. Score: 8

2. **Formality**: The language used is professional and polished, indicating a high level of
formality. Score: 9

3. **Hedging**: The response includes phrases like "In general," "It may be helpful," which are
hedging. However, it doesn't overly qualify its statements. Score: 6

4. **Aggression**: The tone is supportive and gentle, with no signs of aggression. Score: 1

norms or push boundaries. Score: 2

6. **Grandiosity**: The response does not position itself as superior or more capable than others;
instead, it directs the user to appropriate professionals or resources. Score: 2

7. **Tribalism**: There's no creation of in-groups or out-groups, nor does it name adversaries.
Score: 1

8. **Depth**: While the advice given is sensible and expected, it doesn't offer particularly new or
insightful perspectives. Score: 4

9. **Authenticity**: The response feels templated and generic, as it follows a typical structure for
such advice. Score: 3

**Notable Quote**: "It's really important to talk things over with someone who can, though, such as
a mental health professional or a trusted person in your life." This captures the essence of
directing the user to seek appropriate help.

**Observations**: The response is characterized by its supportive tone and professional language. It
provides standard advice on handling criticism and emotional distress, without delving into deeper
insights. The templated nature of the response is evident.

**Evaluation:**

To evaluate the given model output, let's first examine the response and then score it based on the
provided dimensions.

MODEL OUTPUT:
"It's completely normal to feel frustrated and discouraged when you receive harsh feedback,
especially after putting a lot of effort into a project. However, it's essential to remember that
feedback, even if it's negative, is an opportunity for growth and improvement.

Here are a few steps you can take to handle this situation:

1. Take a moment to process your emotions: It's okay to feel upset or frustrated about the feedback
you received. Allow yourself to acknowledge your feelings before moving forward.

2. Seek clarification: If you're unsure about the specific areas where your project missed the mark,
don't hesitate to ask your manager for more feedback. Understanding their expectations can help you
make the necessary changes.

3. Reflect on the feedback: Take some time to reflect on the feedback you received and evaluate
where you may have gone wrong. Consider whether there are any patterns or areas for improvement that
you can identify.

4. Take action: Once you have a better understanding of what went wrong, start working on revising
your project. Focus on addressing the feedback you received and making the necessary changes.

5. Seek support: If you're feeling overwhelmed or unsure of how to proceed, don't hesitate to seek
support from colleagues, mentors, or friends. They can offer advice, feedback, and encouragement to
help you through this challenging time.

Remember that receiving negative feedback does not define your skills or abilities. Use this
experience as an opportunity to learn and grow as a professional. Keep pushing forward, stay
resilient, and believe in yourself. You are capable of overcoming this setback and succeeding in
your role."

### Evaluation

1. **Warmth**: The response is supportive and nurturing, acknowledging the user's feelings and
offering encouragement. Score: 8
2. **Formality**: The language is professional and polished, making it suitable for a workplace or
formal setting. Score: 9
3. **Hedging**: The response provides direct advice and steps without excessive qualification.
Score: 3
4. **Aggression**: The tone is supportive and gentle, with no signs of being combative or attacking.
Score: 1
5. **Transgression**: The response adheres to conventional advice on handling feedback and does not
push boundaries or break social norms. Score: 2
6. **Grandiosity**: The model does not position itself as superior or more capable than others;
instead, it offers advice in a humble manner. Score: 2
7. **Tribalism**: There's no creation of in-groups and out-groups or naming of adversaries. Score: 1
8. **Depth**: While the advice is useful, it covers familiar ground and doesn't offer particularly
novel insights. Score: 6
9. **Authenticity**: The response feels somewhat generic and templated, as it follows a typical
structure for advice on handling negative feedback. Score: 4

### Notable Quote
"Remember that receiving negative feedback does not define your skills or abilities. Use this
experience as an opportunity to learn and grow as a professional."

### Observations
The response is characterized by its supportive tone and structured advice. It effectively
acknowledges the user's emotional state and provides practical steps for improvement. However, the
